    "evidence_text": "Polymers 2024 ,16, 3045 7 of 14\nof the HS is denoted as T hand that of the SS as T s. It can be seen that due to the different\nstructures of the HS, the span of T hvaries significantly among the five PU materials, ranging\nfrom 315◦C to 346◦C. However, as PUs employ the same SS material, the fluctuation range\nof T sis relatively small, ranging from 397◦C to 412◦C. Furthermore, the ordering of T h\nand T sis generally consistent, with PU-M exhibiting the highest T hand T svalues at 346◦C\nand 412◦C, respectively.\nThe dynamic mechanical properties of PUs are illustrated in Figure 4d,e. Due to\nthe larger molecular volume and relatively higher crystallinity of HMDI and MDI, PU-\nHM and PU-M exhibit comparatively higher storage modulus (E′) below −73◦C. PU-H,\nowing to its excellent crystallinity, also demonstrates an elevated storage modulus below\n−73◦C, falling between PU-HM and PU-M. Conversely, PU-IP and PU-T exhibit lower\nE′at low temperatures due to relatively better flexibility and lower crystallinity. The\npeak temperature of the loss factor (tan δ) is typically considered as the material’s glass\ntransition temperature (T g). Figure 4e reveals the T gof PU-H, PU-T, PU-M, PU-HM, and\nPU-IP to be −48.2◦C, 3.0◦C,−20.7◦C, 15.8◦C, and 12.5◦C, respectively, which are\nbasically in agreement with the DSC-derived sequence. The higher symmetry of HDI,\nMDI, and HMDI leads to the increased aggregation of HS, enhanced crystallinity, and\nhigher phase separation degree. Consequently, during the glass transition, there is minimal\nintramolecular friction among the molecular chains, resulting in a lower tan δ(<0.4) for\nPU-H, PU-M, and PU-HM. Conversely, the lower symmetry of the TDI and IPDI molecules\nresults in the reduced aggregation and crystallinity of HS, leading to higher intramolecular\nfriction and higher tan δduring the glass transition. In the temperature range above T gand\nbelow 100◦C, a consistent trend is observed in the values of the tan δ. PU-H and PU-M\ndisplay low tan δvalues (<0.1) due to their superior symmetry and crystallinity. PU-HM,\nwith slightly lower symmetry and crystallinity, exhibits slightly higher tan δ. Conversely,\nPU-T and PU-IP , with the poorest symmetry and minimal HS crystallization, demonstrate\nhigher tan δvalues.\nThe crystalline properties of PUs were investigated via X-ray diffraction (XRD), with\nthe results illustrated in Figure 4f. PU-T, PU-M, PU-HM, and PU-IP exhibited similar peak\npositions and profiles, characterized by a relatively strong peak at 2 θ= 20–23◦and a weaker\npeak near 2 θ= 43◦. The difference is that the peaks of PU-T and PU-IP at 20–23◦are slightly\nweaker, indicative of typical amorphous characteristics, while those of PU-M and PU-HM\nare slightly stronger, indicating a certain degree of crystallinity. Notably, PU-H displayed\nthree distinct peaks between 20 and 26◦, with sharper profiles compared to the other PUs,\nindicating a higher degree of crystallization. At 2 θ= 43◦, PU-H resembled the other four\nPUs, showing a broad, weak peak characteristic of an amorphous phase.\n3.3. Morphology, Wettability, Mechanical Property, and Self-Healing Ability\nAtomic force microscopy (AFM) is a crucial tool for investigating the microphase\nseparation behavior of PU. Figure 5a displays the AFM height images of the five PUs. In the\nfigure, the bright regions represent the HS phase, while the dark regions represent the SS\nphase. It can be observed that the interface between the bright and dark regions in PU-M is\ndistinct, with a uniform and fine distribution of SS and HS, indicating a clear microphase\nseparation. This is mainly attributed to the presence of rigid aromatic rings in the HS phase\nof PU-M, along with the good symmetry of MDI, strong interactions between HS, and\nrelatively good crystallinity, resulting in a more concentrated HS and more pronounced\nphase separation. In the AFM image of PU-HM, the interface between the bright and\ndark regions is somewhat blurred, indicating a slightly lower degree of phase separation\ncompared to PU-M. The larger bright regions in PU-H are mainly attributed to its higher\ncrystallinity. On the other hand, PU-T and PU-IP exhibit the lowest degree of microphase\nseparation, which is attributed to the poorer molecular symmetry of TDI and IPDI and a\nloose accumulation of HS, leading to more interactions between SS and HS, promoting\nmicrophase mixing.",

    "evidence_text": "Materials 2023 ,16, 1072 13 of 22\nmotion (i.e., vibrations and short-range rotations of the soft segment) at such low tempera-\ntures. Therefore, it was expected that the MP 1/2.22/1.22 specimen would exhibit superior\nshape ﬁxation properties because of its higher modulus in the glassy state as in [ 51]. Upon\ncooling further to −50◦C, the value of G′gradually decreased, corresponding to the T gof\nthe soft segment. Beyond this point, the rubbery plateau is reached, wherein the storage\nmodulus increases with an increase in the hard segment content due to the presence of a\nmore crosslinked structure, structural reinforcement, and a high elastic recovery at high\ntemperatures [ 2]. Considering that the storage modulus deﬁnes the energy stored elastically\nby a material at deformation and supplies information regarding the polymer stiffness [ 38],\nthe increased storage modulus observed at higher contents of the hard segment can be\nattributed to the presence of a glassy state due to the crystallinity of this domain. As shown\nin Figure 6b, the highest values of G′were within the range of 3.3–3.5 MPa. In addition,\nthe relative crosslinking densities of the polyurethane species were associated with the\nobserved storage moduli in the rubbery plateau region, wherein a higher crosslinking\ndensity was associated with a greater hard segment content, indicating that the chain\nsegments were more closely restricted by net points to inhibit their mobility. Due to the\nfact that shape recovery in thermoset polymers is dependent on the covalently-bonded net\npoints [ 10], it was, therefore, considered that polymers with greater hard segment contents\nwould exhibit enhanced shape recovery properties.\nTable 7. Thus,\nbased on the results presented in Table 7, it appears that the increase in T ﬂowmay be the\nresult of increased hydrogen bonding in the hard domain [ 35]. In addition, the modulus\nat the rubbery plateau, i.e., in the T ﬂex−Tﬂow, is a function of the crosslinking density\nin the hard segment and reinforcement by the separated hard domain. Importantly, it\nhas been reported that TPUs based on segmented copolymers can be synthesized with a\nrange of rubbery moduli via variation in the hard segment content [ 37]. Upon considering\nour prepared specimens, it can be seen that the rubbery plateau in the MP 1/2.22/1.22 is\nextended compared to that of the MP 1/1.50/1.50 sample, with a higher rubbery modulus\nalso being observed for the former. Furthermore, as suggested above, the relatively stable\nvalue of the storage modulus at low temperatures indicates that that no phase transitions\noccur within this temperature range due to effective phase separation. These results are\nconﬁrmed by the higher Xbobtained for the MP 1/2.22/1.22 specimen following FTIR\nanalysis.     "evidence_text": "Materials 2023 ,16, 1072 13 of 22\nmotion (i.e., vibrations and short-range rotations of the soft segment) at such low tempera-\ntures. Therefore, it was expected that the MP 1/2.22/1.22 specimen would exhibit superior\nshape ﬁxation properties because of its higher modulus in the glassy state as in [ 51]. Upon\ncooling further to −50◦C, the value of G′gradually decreased, corresponding to the T gof\nthe soft segment. Beyond this point, the rubbery plateau is reached, wherein the storage\nmodulus increases with an increase in the hard segment content due to the presence of a\nmore crosslinked structure, structural reinforcement, and a high elastic recovery at high\ntemperatures [ 2]. Considering that the storage modulus deﬁnes the energy stored elastically\nby a material at deformation and supplies information regarding the polymer stiffness [ 38],\nthe increased storage modulus observed at higher contents of the hard segment can be\nattributed to the presence of a glassy state due to the crystallinity of this domain. As shown\nin Figure 6b, the highest values of G′were within the range of 3.3–3.5 MPa. In addition,\nthe relative crosslinking densities of the polyurethane species were associated with the\nobserved storage moduli in the rubbery plateau region, wherein a higher crosslinking\ndensity was associated with a greater hard segment content, indicating that the chain\nsegments were more closely restricted by net points to inhibit their mobility. Due to the\nfact that shape recovery in thermoset polymers is dependent on the covalently-bonded net\npoints [ 10], it was, therefore, considered that polymers with greater hard segment contents\nwould exhibit enhanced shape recovery properties.\nTable 7. Summary of the dynamic mechanical analysis results for the bio-based SMTPUs.\nSample T g(◦C) G′25(MPa)aTﬂex(◦C)bTﬂow(◦C)c\nMP 1/2.22/1.22 −6.7 1.69 16.6 126.7\nMP 1/1.90/0.90 −7.5 1.53 14.5 104.9\nMP 1/1.50/0.50 −13.9 1.23 10.2 69.0\naG′25: Storage modulus at 25◦C.bTﬂex: The ﬂex temperature (temperature at the start of the rubber plateau\nregion).cTﬂow: The ﬂow temperature (the temperature where the storage modulus G′reached 1 MPa).\nThe ﬂex temperature (T ﬂex), which is also provided in Table 7 for the three SMTPU\nspecimens, is deﬁned as the temperature at the start of the rubbery plateau region, while\nTﬂowcorresponds to the point at which melting of the hard segment takes place. Thus,\nbased on the results presented in Table 7, it appears that the increase in T ﬂowmay be the\nresult of increased hydrogen bonding in the hard domain [ 35]. In addition, the modulus\nat the rubbery plateau, i.e., in the T ﬂex−Tﬂow, is a function of the crosslinking density\nin the hard segment and reinforcement by the separated hard domain. Importantly, it\nhas been reported that TPUs based on segmented copolymers can be synthesized with a\nrange of rubbery moduli via variation in the hard segment content [ 37]. These results are\nconﬁrmed by the higher Xbobtained for the MP 1/2.22/1.22 specimen following FTIR\nanalysis.
